# A075 — balanced composition, erasure collisions, and restriction extraction

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For arbitrary Boolean sign f on m uniform coordinates and a balanced Boolean sign g on n uniform coordinates, let F=f(g(x^(1)),...,g(x^(m))) on disjoint blocks. Entropy H is in bits, p_f(T)=fhat(T)^2, I_i is physical influence, I=sum I_i, B_i=H(S_i|S_-i), B=sum B_i. This stage proves exact H, I and B identities, specifies precisely when the B identity needs the no-level-1 hypothesis, and gives a constructive restriction-extraction implication for universal selectable-erasure constants. It also supplies a fully certified recursive Boolean family and an explicit failing restriction. Full FEI remains OPEN. This is a narrow reconstruction/repair of B15's supplied composition calibration, not a new broad universal inequality or a new exhaustive extremizer search.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

Protocol revision 2026-10-11-execution-v2 was read in full; report README, CURRENT_STATUS, registries, packet matrix, stream actions, campaign plan and earlier B05/B09/B19 reports were loaded and inspected. Supplemental CAMPAIGN, CLAIMS, COUNTEREXAMPLES, SEED_LEMMAS, NATIVE_MAPPING and source notes were consulted. BOARD.md was read at stage start; B15 STREAM_NOTES.md v1, 2026-10-11 22:50 UTC directly supplies this target. The retired biased-grid census is not repeated. ERASURE_REDUCTION.md sections 1,2,4 supply the actual restriction identities and scalar JS bound, reconstructed below. A082 REPORT.md at run 20261011T221008Z-account05-A082-finite-inequalities was read for the point-defect constant and scope; only its witness is reconstructed here, not its n<=4 optimality theorem.

All four supplied paper texts are loaded with cp1252 decoding (the files are not UTF-8), and introductions and composition/restriction locators inspected. Their class theorems and percolation hypotheses are context, not premises of this proof. No claim of a full line-by-line paper audit or literature novelty is made. The saved source receipts identify actual file bytes, hashes and relevant excerpts. No external source or unreviewed campaign theorem is needed for the composition calculation.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Write phi(a,b)=-a log2(a)-b log2(b)+(a+b)log2(a+b), with continuous zero conventions. It is homogeneous, nonnegative, and strictly positive exactly when a,b>0. Put q(V)=ghat(V)^2. Since g is balanced, q(empty)=0. Expanding f in its Walsh basis gives, for an index U with nonempty block parts U_j and active-block set T,

    Fhat(U)=fhat(T) product_{j in T} ghat(U_j).

There is one term: inactive blocks contribute the constant 1, whereas an active block has no constant coefficient. Thus the spectral generative law is first T~p_f, then independent nonempty V_j~q for j in T. Its entropy and expected set size give, without any no-level-1 assumption,

    H(F)=H(f)+I(f)H(g),             I(F)=I(f)I(g),
    I_(j,i)(F)=I_j(f) I_i(g).                                      (1)

Fix coordinate i in inner block j. For each residual index with a nonempty remainder V in block j, both adjacent packets come from the same active outer block j. Homogeneity of phi, followed by summing the independent other blocks, gives I_j(f) times the V!=empty portion of B_i(g). The V=empty portion of B_i(g) is phi(q(empty),q({i}))=0. Residual indices with block j empty instead pair an inactive block and a singleton-active block. Consequently the exact formula is

    B_(j,i)(F)=I_j(f) B_i(g)
      + sum_{T subset [m]\{j}} phi(p_f(T), p_f(T union {j}) q({i})). (2)

The correction vanishes iff q({i})=0 or B_j(f)=0. This iff includes irrelevant outer coordinates. Summing proves

    B(F)=I(f)B(g)+sum_{j,i,T} phi(p_f(T),p_f(T+j)q({i})).           (3)

Therefore B(F)=I(f)B(g) iff, for every pair j,i, q({i})=0 or B_j(f)=0. Equivalently either g has no singleton Fourier mass or B(f)=0. This proves the lead's sufficient no-level-1 hypothesis is unnecessary for (1), sufficient but not necessary for (3), and cannot be dropped from (3) without a substitute. Counterexample: g(x)=x and f=AND2; B(g)=0 but F=f and B(F)>0. No incoherent mixture or shared-variable substitution is used.

For balanced nonconstant f with no singleton mass, define f^[0](x)=x and f^[d+1]=f composed with disjoint copies of f^[d]. Set a=I(f), h=H(f), b=B(f). The inner gates remain balanced and have no singleton mass for d>=1; the d=0 identity is immediate. Equations (1)-(3) imply

    I_d=a^d, H_d=h(1+a+...+a^(d-1)), B_d=b a^(d-1).              (4)

Thus for a>1, H_d/I_d -> h/(a-1) and B_d/I_d=b/a, for d>=1. Coordinate influence of a balanced nonconstant function sums to at least one since p(empty)=0. If a=1 and f has no singleton mass, there is a contradiction since then every nonempty supported set would have size>=2. More generally a balanced Boolean function with a=1 has h=0 and is a signed dictator. This handles the excluded denominator exactly.

The zero-erasure alternative also permits recursion despite singleton mass: if f and all recursively composed inner gates have B=0, every correction in (2) vanishes. A concrete example is M=Maj3=(x1+x2+x3-x1*x2*x3)/2. It has four probabilities 1/4, H=2, I=3/2, and every supported set has odd cardinality. Hence B=0. Its recursive functions M_d are odd under global input negation, so only odd Fourier degrees occur and B(M_d)=0. Formula (1) therefore gives for every d>=0,

    I(M_d)=(3/2)^d, H(M_d)=4((3/2)^d-1), B(M_d)=0.              (5)

Their H/I approaches 4, while every root coordinate has zero true erasure. This is a certified all-dimensional family with bounded ratio, not a counterfamily to FEI and not a new FEI class claim.

Here is a physically explicit restriction where all coordinates fail a finite erasure price. Write M_2=Maj3(Maj3(x1,x2,x3),Maj3(x4,x5,x6),Maj3(x7,x8,x9)). Fix

    x3=-1, x5=+1, x6=-1, x7=x8=x9=-1.

The three branches become AND2(x1,x2), x4, and -1. The root is AND3(x1,x2,x4), with output +1 only at +++. Its spectrum has p(empty)=9/16 and seven other probabilities 1/16. For every relevant i,

    I_i=1/4,
    B_i=phi(9/16,1/16)+3 phi(1/16,1/16),
    B_i/I_i=Kstar=4+(5/2)log2(5)-(9/2)log2(3).                 (6)

Thus every coordinate of this named restriction fails B_i<=K I_i whenever K<Kstar. The saved rational interval certifies 267/100<Kstar<107/40. For every d>2 it is also a restriction of M_d: inside every bottom majority gate fix two children to opposite constants, leaving the third child as a dictator, until the remaining formula has depth two. Constant subtrees are implemented by fixing their leaves uniformly. Then apply the six assignments above to the remaining nine representatives. This gives a deterministic physical restriction recipe for all d>=2. Sharpness beyond this particular witness is not asserted.

For clarity, a universal selectable-erasure principle quantifies over every Boolean residual, not merely the root of a family. Let c=2/ln2. Actual restrictions give

    Delta_i H = B_i + JS_i,       0<=JS_i<=c I_i,
    I(f)=I_i(f)+(I(f_+)+I(f_-))/2.                             (7)

The first equality follows packetwise by writing the child coefficients as u+v,u-v and cancelling entropy of u^2+v^2. For the scalar bound, d(z)=((1+z)ln(1+z)+(1-z)ln(1-z))/2 satisfies d(z)<=1-sqrt(1-z^2): differentiate, use atanh(z)<=z/sqrt(1-z^2), then integrate. With z=2|uv|/(u^2+v^2) this gives JS packet <=c min(u^2,v^2). Summing yields (7). The influence identity averages each remaining physical edge; it spends the queried coordinate once.

If every nonconstant Boolean residual has a relevant i with B_i<=K I_i, induction using (7) gives H<=(K+c)I. Hence any such universal K satisfies

    K >= sup_{f nonconstant} H(f)/I(f) - c.                   (8)

This is a necessary lower bound; it does not pay a universal K. It applies to the supremum of min_i B_i/I_i, distinct from the aggregate supremum of B/I. Applying (5) yields K>=4-c. The explicit hereditary witness (6) is stronger for this family: K>=Kstar is forced on its restriction closure.

There is also a constructive extraction theorem. Suppose a finite Boolean F has H(F)/I(F)>K+c, K>=0. Choose rationals k>K and C>k+c with H(F)>C I(F). Maintain the strictly positive potential P=H-C I at a physical residual. If every relevant i has B_i>k I_i, stop: this is the demanded failing residual. Otherwise choose the least relevant i with B_i<=k I_i. By (7), Delta_i H-C I_i<0, so

    (P(f_+)+P(f_-))/2=P(f)-Delta_i H+C I_i>0.

Choose the child with larger P (break ties with +1). It has positive P. Each step fixes a new coordinate, and a constant residual has P=0, so the algorithm must stop before exhausting all coordinates. At its output every relevant i has B_i>k I_i>K I_i. Decisions for rational k,C are exact: Boolean squared coefficients are rational, and every entropy expression is a rational combination of prime logarithms; multiply denominators and compare the resulting positive integers. The rational choices exist by strict slack; a search over rational pairs with outward enclosures finds them. The algorithm is computable, not claimed polynomial-time or memory-efficient in arbitrary dimension. Applied to recursive families with limiting ratio rho, it works for every K<rho-c once d has certified positive initial slack. Our interval check certifies positive slack already for M_10 and K=1. Its 59049-bit truth table was not generated; this is an analytic parameter check. The explicit restriction (6) avoids such a computation altogether for K<Kstar.

The first unsupported universal line is a dimension-independent influence payment on all actual residuals of unrestricted Boolean functions. A top-level B/I composition invariant provides no such payment. Neither (8) nor a finite collection of extracted obstructions proves finiteness or divergence of the universal optimal K.

# A075 stage 2 — sparse matching pattern closed in every dimension

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Following B15 v2, turn the six-bit witness into a Boolean family and decide analytically whether its selectable true erasure grows. For k,l>=0 let r=2k+l, let M be k disjoint coordinate pairs, and set

    A_(k,l)={0,e_1,...,e_r} union {e_i+e_j : {i,j} in M},
    f_(k,l)=1-2*1_A on the uniform physical r-cube.

There are l unmatched coordinates. Characters are (-1)^(x dot S), N=2^r, p(S)=fhat(S)^2, B_i=H(S_i|S_-i), d_i=Inf_i, and E(f)=min_(d_i>0) B_i/d_i. All entropy units are bits. Coordinate permutations, input reflections, output negation and dummy padding preserve the corresponding erasure ratios. General affine variable changes are not used.

The all-dimensional result is sharp, including physical descendants: every relevant coordinate of every member of the restriction closure of these matching functions satisfies

    B_i <= K6 d_i,
    K6=16/3-(21/8)log2(3)+(95/24)log2(5)
                 -(121/24)log2(11)+(65/24)log2(13)
       =2.9444953038718586... .                                  (1)

Equality occurs precisely at the six-variable, three-pair matching root (up to the stated symmetries/dummies), for all six coordinates. In particular the supremum of E and of B/I on this hereditary class is K6. Perfect matching roots f_(k,0) have E->2 and H/I->4. Thus this pattern is not an unbounded-ratio Boolean counterfamily. Separate root bounds show arbitrary graph-edge variants and padded monotone paths also have E<=2+o(1), uniformly as dimension grows. Those separate root estimates are not silently promoted to a hereditary tree theorem or full FEI.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

Read BOARD.md snapshot 2026-10-13 21:24 UTC, B15 STREAM_NOTES.md v2 (2026-10-11 23:25 UTC), own STATUS/LEARNED and the complete execution protocol/2 revision 2026-10-11-execution-v2. Read B15 lead CLAIMS.json, B15-LKSTAR-FAILS-N5-N6@1 and its composition calibration, A071 stage2 REPORT.md (exact five-bit maximum and 640 path extremizers), A072 stage2 REPORT.md (exact six-bit prime-log expression), A074 stage2 REPORT.md (independent finite witness reconstruction), and A089 stage2 REPORT.md (raw five-bit recursion bounded and continuity failure for newly relevant coordinates). A074's path was found by file inventory after one guessed directory was absent. No other agent's code was executed or imported.

Prior-stage protocol/paper/source consultation is retained. The threshold and percolation class theorems are context, not premises. This stage uses the exact six-bit witness and the newly classified five-bit path geometry rather than repeating any all-function census. All source bytes/hashes and consultation scopes are saved in out/SOURCE_RECEIPTS.json. Source identities C005/ERASURE_REDUCTION are re-derived where used. Novelty is not claimed. This is the lead-directed closure of an existing witness mechanism; it introduces no broad universal candidate despite the pending-claim cap.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### Spectrum, physical influence and exact erasure formula

Put a=|A|=1+3k+l. On a Fourier index S, let u be the number of matched pairs intersecting S and v the number of isolated coordinates in S. For one pair the indicator character sum contribution is t_i+t_j+t_i*t_j, where t_i=(-1)^(1_(i in S)): it is 3 when neither coordinate occurs and -1 otherwise. Each isolated coordinate contributes 1 or -1. Therefore

    L_(u,v)=a-4u-2v,
    multiplicity C(k,u)3^u C(l,v),
    fhat(empty)=1-2a/N, fhat(S)=-2 L_(u,v)/N for S!=empty.       (2)

The empty index is the one exceptional element of the (u,v)=(0,0) class. A paired physical direction has exactly two internal A-edges, 0--e_i and e_partner--(e_i+e_partner). An isolated direction has one internal edge. Thus the respective crossing-edge counts are a-4 and a-2. If t_i is 2 for paired and 1 for isolated directions, put delta_i=a-2t_i; then

    d_i=2 delta_i/N, fhat({i})=-2 delta_i/N.                   (3)

The second identity also follows from counting A points with bit i=1. Irrelevant directions are omitted; low-dimensional constant cases cause no division.

Write Phi(x,y)=(x+y)log2(x+y)-x log2(x)-y log2(y), using continuous zero conventions. Let G(x,y) denote the same expression on unnormalized masses. The paired-coordinate residual packets, with u counting the OTHER k-1 pairs and v counting the l isolated coordinates, have weight C(k-1,u)3^u C(l,v). They are

    (4L^2,4(L-4)^2), partner absent;
    (4(L-4)^2,4(L-4)^2), partner present,
    L=a-4u-2v.                                                (4)

For u=v=0 in the first packet replace 4L^2 by (N-2a)^2. The paired B_i is the weighted sum of G over (4), divided by N^2. For an isolated coordinate use u=0..k, v=0..l-1, weight C(k,u)3^u C(l-1,v), and packets (4L^2,4(L-2)^2), with the identical empty-packet replacement. These formulas are exact finite logarithmic sums in parameters; no large-dimensional truth table is required. Pair swaps/pair permutations and isolated-coordinate permutations prove there are at most two physical coordinate orbits.

### Actual restriction closure

If all fixed bits are zero, surviving intact pairs remain pairs, and a broken pair leaves its free member isolated. This gives another A_(k',l'). If exactly one fixed bit is one, belonging to coordinate j, the only negative residual points are 0 and possibly e_partner(j), provided that partner is free. This is a point defect with at most one dummy coordinate. If exactly two fixed ones form a matching pair, only the zero residual point is negative; otherwise the residual is constant +1. Three fixed ones also force +1. Full negative residual cubes in very small dimensions are constants -1. Further restrictions of a point defect are point defects or constants, with dummy coordinates unchanged. This proves the complete hereditary classification, for simultaneous assignments as well as one-step slices.

### All-dimensional cutoff and sharp certificate

For ANY Boolean sign with negative side size a, let q0=(1-2a/N)^2 and q_i=fhat({i})^2. Every erasure packet satisfies Phi(x,y)<=x+y. All packets except the empty remainder therefore contribute at most 1-q0-q_i. Moreover

    Phi(x,y)<=y log2(e(x+y)/y),

by x log2(1+y/x)<=y/ln2. Since q0+q_i<=1,

    B_i <= 1-q0-q_i+q_i log2(e/q_i).                         (5)

For (3), q_i=4delta_i^2/N^2. Using 1-q0<=4a/N, log2(e)<3/2, and dropping negative logarithms gives

    B_i/d_i <= 2a/delta_i+(2delta_i/N)(2r+3/2).

Here delta_i>=r-3 and delta_i<=a<=1+3r/2. Also 2a/delta_i=2+4t_i/delta_i<=2+8/(r-3). Consequently for r>3,

    B_i/d_i <= U(r)=2+8/(r-3)+(3r+2)(2r+3/2)/2^r.           (6)

At r=13, U=240651/81920<368/125<K6. It decreases thereafter. The rational term decreases directly. For P(r)=(3r+2)(2r+3/2),

    2P(r)-P(r+1)=6r^2-(7/2)r-23/2>0 for r>=13,

so P(r)/2^r decreases. The positivity follows already at 13 and from its positive derivative. No asymptotic extrapolation is needed to rule out equality in r>=13.

The finite remaining parameters 1<=r<=12 have only 48 nonzero-dimensional (k,l) pairs, at most two coordinate orbits per pair. Exact packets (4) and outward logarithm intervals certify every relevant orbit strictly below K6, except (k,l)=(3,0), whose exact rational prime-log form equals (1). There are six equality coordinate rows; the saved certificate verifies equality symbolically, not by overlapping intervals. Parameters r<=9 were reconciled against full signed truth tables, integer Walsh transforms, direct physical edges and all 350 single-coordinate children. The additional r=10,11,12 cutoff consists of only 19 compressed parameter pairs and 33 coordinate orbits. It closes a proved finite-to-infinite cutoff, not a census of arbitrary functions.

Point-defect descendants in s variables have d=2^(1-s),

    B_i/d = 2-2d+Phi((1-d)^2,d^2)/d
          <=2+d(2s-4+log2 e).                               (7)

For s=1 erasure is zero. For s=2 the rough bound is <11/4. For s>=3 the term 2^(1-s)(2s-5/2) is largest at s=3, so (7)<23/8<368/125. Dummy coordinates are irrelevant. This completes (1) on every physical descendant and proves sharpness globally within the stated class. This is an all-dimensional theorem supplied by exact finite certificates plus an analytic tail; the finite passing domain is never itself called universal.

It also gives a terminal-inclusive restriction payment. On ANY physical stopping tree starting in this class,

    sum_v w_v B_(i_v)(f_v) <= K6 [I(f)-E I(f_leaf)].

Indeed every descendant belongs to the classified closure and I telescopes by the ordinary physical influence identity. From Delta_i H=B_i+JS_i and JS_i<=(2/ln2)d_i,

    H(f)<=E H(f_leaf)+(K6+2/ln2)[I(f)-E I(f_leaf)].           (8)

This retains all terminal entropy and never reuses an ancestral coordinate's influence. Complete restriction trees have constant leaves, giving H<=(K6+2/ln2)I on the class. This is a scoped payment, not an improvement to an all-Boolean constant; the stronger point is the sharp erasure bottleneck and hereditary closure.

### Perfect matching limits, not a conjecture counterfamily

For l=0, r=2k, a=3k+1 and delta=a-4, every coordinate is interchangeable, so E=B_i/d_i. A scalar consequence of the C005 entropy bound is

    x^2+y^2-Phi(x^2,y^2) <= (|x|-|y|)^2/ln2.                (9)

To reconstruct it, set z=(x^2-y^2)/(x^2+y^2) and use

    ((1+z)ln(1+z)+(1-z)ln(1-z))/2 <= 1-sqrt(1-z^2).

Both sides vanish at zero; their derivatives obey atanh(z)<=z/sqrt(1-z^2) on [0,1], and evenness handles negative z. Substituting gives (9). In (4), partner-present packets have equal masses, while partner-absent amplitude differences have absolute value at most 8/N. There are N/4 such packets; omit the exceptional empty packet and sum the remaining deficits. Their total is at most 16/(N ln2). Therefore

    2a/delta-2(a^2+delta^2)/(delta N)-8/(delta ln2)
       <= E(f_(k,0))
       <= 2a/delta+(2delta/N)(2r+3/2).                       (10)

Both endpoints tend to 2. This proves E->2 and excludes divergence without relying on the frozen k=2..32 scan. The scan's maximum is at k=3, and (1) independently proves that maximum for all k.

For completeness let T=1-q0=4a/N(1-a/N). Every nonempty coefficient has integer numerator -2L, |L|<=a. Thus

    T(2r-2-2log2 a) <= H(f) <= T(2r-2+1/ln2),

where the empty entropy is nonnegative and <=T/ln2. Divide by I=2r delta/N. Since a/delta->1, a/N->0 and log(a)/r->0, both bounds tend to 4. Consequently H/I->4. The entropy-to-influence growth and local erasure behavior are different quantities: neither diverges here.

### Quarantine the adjacent sparse mechanisms

The same mass bound (5) rules out graph-edge extensions as a large-dimensional erasure mechanism. For any simple graph G on n physical coordinates, set A={0,all e_i,all edge pairs}. Then a=1+n+e, and a direction of degree d_i^G has singleton coefficient -2delta_i/N and influence 2delta_i/N, delta_i=a-2(1+d_i^G). Choose a minimum-degree vertex. Since d_min<=2e/n,

    delta>=a-2-4e/n>=a(1-4/n)>0 for n>4.

Hence, uniformly over ALL simple graphs,

    E(f_G)<=2+8/(n-4)+(n^2+n+2)(2n+3/2)/2^n.                (11)

This tends to 2 from above and is <368/125 already at n=16: its value there is 551825/196608. The polynomial tail decreases for n>=16 because 2Q(n)-Q(n+1)=2n^3-(5/2)n^2-(15/2)n-8>0 for Q(n)=(n^2+n+2)(2n+3/2). Thus no graph in any dimension >=16 beats the existing six-bit witness. Dimensions <=15 are bounded by elementary granularity (every relevant influence>=2^(1-n)) and B_i<=h2(d_i)<=d_i log2(e/d_i), yielding E<31/2. No exact small-graph extremizer beyond our frozen domain is claimed. Root graph estimates do not supply arbitrary descendant payment after missing singleton points or shifted support occur.

For the five-bit pattern, let P_(n,m) have negative side {0,e1,e1+e2,...,e1+...+e_m}, 0<=m<=n. Its size is a=m+1. Each active direction i<=m has one internal edge, so d_i=2(m-1)/N and singleton numerator -2c_i with c_i=2i-m-1. Every outside direction has d_i=2a/N and singleton numerator -2a. If m<n choose an outside direction. Equation (5) gives

    E(P_(n,m))<=2+2(n+1)(2n+3/2)/2^n.                       (12)

If m=n>=2 choose a middle active coordinate with |c_i|<=1. With q_i=0 or 4/N^2, (5) gives

    E(P_(n,n))<=2+4/(n-1)+2(2n+3/2)/((n-1)2^n).            (13)

Both bounds tend to 2 uniformly over path length/padding. For n>=10 their maximum is <368/125, with the two cutoff values 2521/1024 and 22571/9216. Monotonicity follows from the positive differences of the quadratic tails (for (12), 2P(n)-P(n+1)=4n^2-n-8) and the decreasing rational term in (13). The m=0 point-defect cases are included in (12); m=n=1 is constant. For n<=9, granularity gives E<19/2. Thus padded/long paths cannot give unbounded erasure either. The original f5 is a translated/permuted member with (n,m)=(5,2). Equations (11)-(13) are root upper bounds, not equality limits, and do not close universal selection.

### First unsupported line and changed mechanism

The sparse matching route is now resolved; its natural graph/path extensions also cannot grow E with dimension. The first unsupported universal line is a uniform payment for arbitrary Boolean descendants, including residual support geometry that is not polynomially sparse and biases produced by composition. Equations (1), (8), (11) and (13) cannot be substituted for that missing quantifier.

NEXT changes mechanism to the biased signed-collision admission ledger with the TRANSITIVE six-bit seed as outer gate and an arbitrary level-one-carrying inner core. Raw biased recursion of f6 is a calibration, not the universal target: fixed-gate read-once class results already explain why closing that recursion alone would be insufficient. A089 already closed raw f5 recursion by finding a cheap path through two coordinate types, so that mechanism is not repeated. The six-bit matching gate is transitive; identical disjoint recursion remains transitive on all leaves, leaving one coordinate orbit and preventing the same cheap-orbit selection escape. If p is the probability of a child output encoded as bit 1, its exact minority-output probability and one outer physical derivative probability are

    alpha(p)=(1-p)^4(1+4p-2p^2),
    q(p)=2p(1-p)^3(2-p).

These follow by counting zero, singleton and matching-pair inputs and the boundary patterns. They give only output bias/influence; the unsigned mean recursion cannot pay true erasure. The next mathematical action is to insert this gate in the unequal-mean signed-fiber collision ledger for an arbitrary inner Boolean g, retaining the changing empty row, inherited B_i(g), every newly relevant coordinate and full terminal H(g). The concrete open payment is a normalized collision reserve that is charged once to physical influence without assuming the inner FEI bound. Raw f6 profiles can falsify the proposed reserve but cannot prove its universal validity. No all-depth erasure/admission claim for this new mechanism is made here.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

The first unsupported universal line remains a finite dimension-independent payment of inherited core erasure plus retained arbitrary-core terminal entropy for a universal selection policy, or a common exclusive reserve for two cores sharing physical coordinates. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

An elementary induction proves nonzero degree-r F2 polynomials have weight >=2^-r; derivatives give eta=2^(1-d) for degree-d sign branches. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

The remaining combined account is explicitly FEI-strength: for balanced arbitrary G,H=1, a uniform A_T<=K Pi on one complete core tree per G implies FEI with C=2K+4/ln2. A combined signed child-surplus/core-influence payment at the second collision, and a genuinely shared queried coordinate, remain open.

# A077 / B16: a multiscale surcharge failure with exact KL cancellation

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Entropy and relative entropy are in bits; all physical inputs are independent fair bits and outputs are signs. This stage tests the specific candidate that every complete one-coordinate restriction tree can pay its cumulative **blind** minority-mismatch surcharge

\[
R_{\mathcal T}=\sum_v w_v M_v\log_2(1/M_v)\le K I(f)
\]

with one absolute finite K. Here parent Fourier masses in a residual fiber are u_T^2,v_T^2; q_T=u_T^2+v_T^2, m_T=min(u_T^2,v_T^2), M=sum m_T, and nu_T=m_T/M. These are the exact minority masses of the pinned isospectral-splicing interface, not ordinary disagreement unless explicitly calculated. Zero-M contributions vanish.

For every n>=1 and every ordered partition n=r_1+...+r_L into positive integers, take mutually disjoint fresh physical blocks z^(j), A_j=1{z^(j)=0}, F_0=-1, F_j=1+A_j(F_(j-1)-1). Then

\[
F_L=1-2\prod_j A_j=f_n=1-2\mathbf1_{\{x=0\}}.
\]

The probability of the defect after scale j is 2^(-r_1-...-r_j). No controls are reused. The nested changes overlap completely on the final defect, so entropy additivity across activation scales would be unjustified; the product-indicator expansion below computes this interference exactly.

Result: for any complete physical one-coordinate restriction tree on this function, skipping irrelevant queries,

\[
R_{\mathcal T}=2^{-n}n(n-1),\qquad I(f_n)=2n2^{-n},\qquad R_{\mathcal T}/I=(n-1)/2.
\]

Thus no finite K pays this candidate, even by optimizing the queried coordinate order. In contrast the exact corrected charge

\[
Q_{\mathcal T}=\sum_v w_v M_v[\log_2(1/M_v)-D_2(\nu_v\Vert q_v)]
\]

satisfies Q<=2I, Q/I->1, and H(f_n)<=4I with H/I->4. This is an intermediate overcharge counterfamily, not a counterexample to FEI or to the retained-KL stopping-tree target. Independent review is pending; no novelty or formal certification is claimed.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

BOARD.md was read at stage start (snapshot 2026-10-11 22:05 UTC). B16/STREAM_NOTES.md is absent at the supplied path, so no lead direction was available. There were no existing rolling files in this agent folder.

SEED_LEMMAS Section 3 already supplies the point-defect spectrum and Shannon asymptotic; those are calibration, not claimed discoveries. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Write p=2^-n. Every indicator coefficient is p, since the selected point is zero in binary Walsh coordinates. Consequently fhat(empty)=1-2p and fhat(S)=-2p for every nonempty S. In particular all cross-scale signs and coefficients are retained. Parseval is (1-2p)^2+(2^n-1)4p^2=1. Each coordinate influence is 2p, by counting the single disagreeing undirected edge or summing its 2^(n-1) spectral masses. Thus I=2np. The exact entropy is

\[
H=(1-2p)^2\log_2\frac1{(1-2p)^2}+8p(1-p)(n-1).
\]

Let v=4p(1-p). The empty term is -(1-v)log2(1-v)<=v/ln2, by -ln(1-v)<=v/(1-v), with endpoint continuity. Hence

\[
H/I\le (1-p)[4(n-1)/n+2/(n\ln2)]\le4.
\]

The n=1 case has H=0 and I=1. As n grows the empty term divided by I vanishes and the tail ratio tends to 4.

Now reveal any physical coordinate. A restriction missing the defect is constant +1; the sole surviving branch is f_(n-1). For any adaptive choice of subsequent coordinate there remains exactly one nonconstant node at each residual dimension m, with history probability w_m=2^(-(n-m)). The labels of the queried coordinates may change but the residual point-defect spectrum does not. All complete coordinate trees therefore have the same ledger. At m=1 the function is a character, so M=0, B=JS=0, while the queried ordinary influence is one.

For m>=2 write d=2^(-(m-1)) and p_m=d/2. The parent residual fiber with T empty has

\[
u_0=1-d,\quad v_0=-d,\quad q_0=(1-d)^2+d^2=1-2d+2d^2;
\]

each of the 1/d-1 other fibers has u_T=v_T=-d and q_T=2d^2. Since d<=1/2, every minority mass is d^2, including the tie at m=2. Hence M=d and nu is uniform with mass d on each residual fiber. This is also the ordinary queried influence at m>=2. The raw weighted charge of this one level is

\[
w_m M\log_2(1/M)=2^{1-n}(m-1).
\]

Summing m=2,...,n yields R=2^-n n(n-1). The same final rarity is used repeatedly in the **surprisal multiplier**: its weighted minority mass is 2^(1-n) at every surviving level. The ordinary influence allocation itself is legitimate and nonreusing, sum_(m=1)^n w_m Inf=2n2^-n; it is the blind surcharge that repeatedly prices the unresolved rarity.

For the rebate, directly compute

\[
D_2(\nu\Vert q)=(1-d)\log_2\frac1{2d}+d\log_2\frac d{q_0},
\]

\[
a_m:=\log_2(1/d)-D_2(\nu\Vert q)
=1-d+2d(m-1)+d\log_2 q_0.
\]

Equivalently M a_m=sum_T m_T log2(q_T/m_T). Each q_T>=2m_T, so a_m>=1. Since q_0<=1 and l/2^l<=1/2 for every integer l>=1 (by induction), a_m<=1+2d(m-1)<=2. Therefore

\[
Q=2^{1-n}\sum_{m=2}^n a_m\le2I,
\quad
1-1/n\le Q/I\le1+3/n.
\]

The last upper bound follows by dropping negative terms in a_m and using sum_(l>=1) l/2^l=2: sum a_m<=n-1+4=n+3. Thus Q/I->1. For n=1 Q=0. The corrected tree charge has a finite price without borrowing a separate ancestor influence reserve. The discarded total KL credit is exactly R-Q, so its leading term is the entire quadratic raw overcharge.

The exact erasure at residual m is

\[
B_m=2d(1-d)+q_0h_2(d^2/q_0).
\]

The residual marginal q consists of q_0 and 1/d-1 copies of 2d^2. Therefore JS_m=H(q)-H(f_(m-1))/2 and Delta_m=H(f_m)-H(f_(m-1))/2=B_m+JS_m. Weighted by w_m, these telescope to H(f_n); terminal entropy is zero. This exact telescope is verified independently of the raw surcharge identities in the checker.

For disjoint tensor factors product_j f_(n_j), spectra are independent, so H and I add. A sequential tree that completes one factor before the next has additive R and Q: a remaining independent tensor factor multiplies q and m by its spectral masses, preserving M and cancelling from nu/q and hence from KL. Both signs at completed leaves merely translate or preserve spectra. Thus

\[
R=\sum_j2^{-n_j}n_j(n_j-1),\quad I=\sum_j2n_j2^{-n_j},\quad Q\le2I,\quad H\le4I.
\]

No such additivity is asserted for overlapping defect flips. Nested activations here were collapsed by their actual Boolean identity, not treated as disjoint spectral pieces.

The first unsupported line toward full FEI would be: every Boolean function admits a proper physical stopping tree whose retained-KL Q and terminal entropy are O(I). This calculation proves that statement only for the explicit point-defect towers and their disjoint tensor products. It neither proves it universally nor exhibits failure of the retained-KL charge on any other family.

# A077 stage 2 — majority's scalar child-defect surcharge diverges

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For odd n, f=Maj_n(x)=sign(sum_i x_i), with independent uniform physical signs. At a queried node v with actual Boolean children g,h, let j_v=min(Pr(g!=h),Pr(g=h)), kappa(g)=1-max_S ghat(S)^2 and kappa_min=min(kappa(g),kappa(h)). In bits define the LOG_ALLOCATION Section 5 scalar bill

\[
\Lambda_{\mathcal T}=\sum_{v\text{ internal}}w_vj_v\log_2(1+\kappa_{\min,v}/(2j_v)),
\]

with zero-j summands zero. T is any complete, deterministic adaptive single-coordinate physical restriction tree, querying only unfixed coordinates and stopping when constant. Randomized orders satisfy the same result by averaging. Define Lambda_noniso by deleting the bill of every node whose two child squared Fourier laws agree, while still continuing the complete restrictions.

For every odd n>=511 and every such tree,

\[
\frac{\Lambda}{I}\ge\frac{n+1}{4n}\log_2\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{3n+1}}{20}\right)
\ge\frac18\log_2n-1,
\]

\[
\frac{\Lambda_{\rm noniso}}{I}\ge\frac{n+1}{8n}\log_2\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{3n+1}}{20}\right)
\ge\frac1{16}\log_2n-\frac12.
\]

For every odd n>=3,

\[
\Lambda_{\rm noniso}\le\Lambda\le I\frac{n-1}{n}\log_2(1/a_n)
\le\frac I2\log_2(2(n-1)),\qquad
a_n=2^{-(n-1)}\binom{n-1}{(n-1)/2},\ I=na_n.
\]

Thus both complete-tree scalar bills are Theta(I log n). This is an exact all-dimensional failure of the proposed scalar **complete-tree** linear payment, including the proposed isospectral deletion repair. The true H/I is bounded for majority by the supplied threshold theorem. Stopping at a majority leaf with an external threshold certificate is permitted and is not refuted; complete-tree failure is not a failure of all stopping schemes. The sharper numerical leading coefficient 1/2 remains unproved by this lower bound. Full FEI remains OPEN.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Exact monotone coefficient maximum

For any monotone Boolean g, in physical sign coordinates,

\[
\widehat g(S)=\mathbb E[ D_i g\,\chi_{S\setminus\{i\}}],\quad
D_i g=(g(x_i=1)-g(x_i=-1))/2\in\{0,1\},\quad i\in S.
\]

Consequently |ghat(S)|<=Inf_i(g), while ghat({i})=Inf_i(g). Thus

\[
\max_{S\ne\varnothing}|\widehat g(S)|=\max_i\operatorname{Inf}_i(g),\quad
\kappa(g)=1-\max\{(\mathbb Eg)^2,\max_i\operatorname{Inf}_i(g)^2\}.
\]

Input sign changes give the same statement for unate functions with absolute singleton coefficients. This sharpens the lead's weaker inequality ghat(S)^2<=Inf_i(g). It needs no Krawtchouk maximum search.

### 2. Elementary central-binomial bounds

Put c_k=2^-k binom(k,floor(k/2)). For r>=1,

\[
\frac1{2\sqrt r}\le c_{2r}\le\frac1{\sqrt{3r+1}}.
\]

The upper bound starts at c_0=1 and the recurrence c_(2r+2)/c_(2r)=(2r+1)/(2r+2) preserves it because

\[
\frac{(2r+1)^2}{(2r+2)^2}\le\frac{3r+1}{3r+4}.
\]

After cross multiplication the right minus left numerator is r. The lower bound starts at r=1 and is preserved since the same ratio squared is >=r/(r+1), with positive cross-multiplied numerator 1. Also c_(2r+1)=c_(2r+2). Hence for k>=1,

\[
c_k^2\le\frac2{3k+2},\qquad c_k\le\frac1{\sqrt{k+1}}.
\]

For even N>=2, c_N>=1/sqrt(2N). These are inequalities of rational squares; no unproved numerical constant is used.

### 3. Physical states and pivotal-bridge mass

After d reveals, set m=n-d and let s be their sum. The residual function is sign(s+X_1+...+X_m). Any adaptive fresh coordinate order has the same distribution of the first d revealed signs: independent fair signs. At d<=(n-1)/2 all nodes are still nonconstant, since |s|<=d<m. The queried disagreement is

\[
q_d(s)=\Pr(X_1+\cdots+X_{m-1}=-s)
=2^{-(m-1)}\binom{m-1}{(m-1-s)/2}.
\]

For m>=2, q<=c_(m-1)<=1/2, so j=q. Summing over s gives E j=a_n by Vandermonde. Weighting a node by its j instead of merely its history probability conditions the total of N=n-1 signs to zero. The first d signs then form an exchangeable balanced sampling bridge. Symmetry gives E X_i=0; expanding (sum_(i=1)^N X_i)^2=0 gives E X_iX_j=-1/(N-1) for i!=j. Therefore

\[
\mathbb E[S_d^2\mid S_N=0]=\frac{d(N-d)}{N-1}.
\]

For d<=N/2 and m=N+1-d this variance is <=m/2: the ratio d(N-d)/[(N-1)(N+1-d)] is increasing up to d=N/2, where the desired inequality reduces to N^2<=(N-1)(N+2), valid for N>=2. Chebyshev yields

\[
\mathbb E_s[j_d(s)\mathbf1_{\{s^2\le m\}}]\ge a_n/2.
\]

There are L=(n+1)/2 depths d=0,...,(n-1)/2, so the probability-weighted good-node mass is at least L a_n/2=(n+1)a_n/4>=I/4. All history probabilities and all queried influence are included. No improbable branch is used without its weight.

### 4. Uniform kappa on good nodes

At a good node s^2<=m, either child has k=m-1 remaining signs and shift t=s+/-1, hence |t|<=sqrt(m)+1. There are no ties, since k+t is odd. By symmetry, the absolute child mean equals the probability that its Rademacher sum lies strictly between -|t| and |t|, an interval containing |t| support atoms. Each atom has probability at most c_k. Thus

\[
|\mathbb Eg|^2\le(\sqrt m+1)^2c_{m-1}^2
\le\frac{2(m+2\sqrt m+1)}{3m-1}\le\frac45\quad(m\ge256).
\]

The last inequality is equivalent to m-10sqrt(m)-7>=0, which is positive at 256 and increasing thereafter. Each child influence is at most c_(m-2)<=1/2 when k>=2; its squared nonempty Fourier maximum is therefore <=1/4 by Section 1. Consequently kappa(g),kappa(h)>=1/5. If n>=511, every first-half residual m is >=256, so this holds at every good node.

The queried j also satisfies j<=c_(m-1)<=sqrt(2/(3m-1)). Therefore each good node logarithm is at least

\[
\log_2(1+\tfrac1{10}\sqrt{(3m-1)/2})
\ge\log_2(1+\sqrt{3n+1}/20).
\]

Multiply by the good mass from Section 3 to obtain the first claimed lower bound. Since sqrt(3n+1)/20>=sqrt(n)/16, the convenient bound (1/8)log2(n)-1 follows. No child entropy or independent tensor spectator is paying this bill.

### 5. Removing every exact isospectral split

At a nonconstant majority state with j>0, child means satisfy mu_+-mu_-=2j>0. Equality of their squared means requires mu_++mu_-=0, equivalently zero parent mean. For a nonconstant majority threshold with the allowed parity this occurs exactly when s=0. Conversely at s=0 the children satisfy g_-(x)=-g_+(-x), so their squared Fourier laws agree. Hence the active exactly isospectral nodes are precisely s=0. Zero-j nodes contribute nothing anyway.

For d even, the pivotal-bridge probability of s=0 is

\[
\Pr(S_d=0\mid S_N=0)=c_d c_{N-d}/c_N\le2/\sqrt{d+1}\quad(0\le d\le N/2).
\]

For odd d it is zero. The displayed bound follows from Section 2 and N-d>=N/2, and remains valid at d=0. Summing over all these depths, even if we overcount odd ones,

\[
\sum_{d=0}^{L-1}\mathbb E[j_d\mathbf1_{s=0}]\le4a_n\sqrt L,
\]

because sum_(u=1)^L u^-1/2<=2sqrt(L), by integration on each interval [u-1,u]. For n>=511 we have L>=256, hence 4sqrt(L)<=L/4. Subtracting this from the good mass L a_n/2 leaves at least L a_n/4=(n+1)a_n/8>=I/8 at good **nonisospectral** nodes. The same logarithm lower bound proves the second theorem. This deletes summands only; it does not grant free arbitrary leaf certificates.

### 6. Matching logarithmic upper order

At every depth with m>=2, j<=1/2 and kappa_min<=1, so

\[
j\log_2(1+\kappa_{\min}/(2j))\le j\log_2(1/j).
\]

After padding constant branches by ignored zero-cost queries, E j=a_n at each depth d=0,...,n-2. Concavity of x log2(1/x) gives expected cost <=a_n log2(1/a_n) at each depth. At residual dimension one, the possible nonconstant threshold is a character and j=0. Thus Lambda<=(n-1)a_n log2(1/a_n). The lower central-binomial bound a_n>=1/sqrt(2(n-1)) proves the asserted upper bound. Complete adaptive coordinate choice changes neither the pivotal distribution nor these bounds.

The first unsupported universal line remains a hereditary retained-profile or actual-erasure transport rule that pays both physical descendants and terminal entropy on arbitrary Boolean cores. This report refutes replacing that transport with the scalar Lambda bill on complete trees, even after its isospectral deletion. It does not claim the exact asymptotic coefficient, a failure for every certified stopping rule, or any unbounded H/I.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

    B_i(F)=[B_i(G)+B_i(H)]/2+D_i, |D_i|<=2Inf_i(F),

The factor two is sharp; D may be negative. Exact terminal entropy is half both core entropies plus a two-frequency potential R. # A077 stage5: repeated private collisions paid by one geometric mark

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For every even k>=2,r>=1 in the previous actual common-activated bent/spike family W, every finite adaptive fresh-coordinate tree may interleave private data, shared data, controls and selector revelation and stop at arbitrary leaves. # complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

The exact greedy/rank integral M(f)=integral_0^{1/2}(m-rank(span{v:d_f(v)<=u}))du shows M(f tensor g)=M(f)+M(g): tensor autocorrelation factors, a mixed direction costs at least each component, and each sublevel span is the direct sum of factor sublevel spans.
